Could use "Frank-Wolfe with exact line search" on the current weighted subgradient: linearize F at a: L(a+d)=F(a)+g^T d (where g is subgradient, e.g., from active max). Since objective is not convex but local. We can choose d as convex combination to a vertex e_j: a_new = (1-α)a+α e_j. The linear decrease in g^T d = α(g^T e_j - g^T a)= α(min_i g_i - weighted_mean_g). Choose j = argmin g_i. Then do line search over α∈[0,1] minimizing actual M or φ. This is very simple, uses one FFT for c/g plus a few trial FFTs for line search. It can move mass to low-gradient coordinates, reducing peaks. This is the standard FW for convex max-of-quadratic? `update_c_pair(c, a, q, j, t)`:
Given current c and a before move. Move t from q to j: a_q-=t, a_j+=t. Need update c. Formula with direction d_q=-1,d_j=1, t amount:

```

ext[:n] = a  # a_{k} for k=0..n-1

```

But note if moving t, a_q old; linear uses old a. If t=a_q (set q to zero), formula still exact? Yes polynomial exact. Need nonnegative t ≤ a_q and if q/j? If q=j invalid. Bounds: t_low = -a_j (negative move j->q), t_high = a_q (positive q->j). We'll use positive.
After update, a[j]+=t; a[q]-=t.
To avoid constructing full S_j for every pair, we can compute `S_j` and `S_q` on fly by slicing. For line search with multiple t, we can compute L = 2*(S_j - S_q) once per pair (O(n) zeros). Then for each t, c_trial = c + t*L + t^2*B. We can vectorize.

`pair_line_search(c, L, B, t_min, t_max, mode)`:

- Candidates: 0, t_max, and geometric or uniform. Since objective convex-ish, use golden section? But nonconvex. - To reduce cost, we can first coarse 16 points, then 16 around best. Also t=0.
- Use exact max M, not smooth. But pair move may increase max due boundary even if weighted derivative negative. - We can also optimize smooth objective φ to reduce active weights; but exact max is goal. Use exact max for acceptance; current_m may not decrease often if top decreases but another rises. Pair selection:
Given s (half gradient). Source candidates: top `q` with largest s. Destination: smallest s. But if s values all similar, choose q with high a and j low. We can generate list of Q sources (e.g., 8) and J dests (e.g., 8), try pairs. Pseudo:
```
def direct_descent(a_init, time_limit, tau=200, K=256, mode='mix', refresh=5, max_moves_per_block=12, random_seed=0, verbose=False):
    n=len(a)
    a=norm_sum1(a)
    c=conv_square(a)
    cur_m=c.max()
    best_a=a.copy(); best_m=cur_m
    t0=time.monotonic()
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    no_improve_moves=0
    update_count=0
    for block in range(...):
        if time > limit: break
        if update_count % refresh ==0:
            # recompute s using soft weights from c
            p=soft_weights(c,tau,K)
            s=gradient_from_weights(p,a)
            # maybe remove mean? not needed for pair
        # choose directions
        tried=0
        accepted=False
        while tried < max_moves_per_block and time < limit:
            # generate candidate moves
            candidates=[]
            if mode in ('fw','mix'):
                # choose low s indices: lowest s (and maybe endpoints)
                idx = np.argsort(s)[:10]  # O(n log n) per move? too costly if per candidate. Do once per block.
                for j in low_list: candidates.append(('fw', None, j))
            if mode in ('pair','mix'):
                # high sources top 10, low dest bottom 10
                for q in high_list:
                    for j in low_list small: candidates.append(('pair', q, j))
                # peak-specific candidate: top 3 peaks
                for k in top_peaks:
                    # valid i for k
                    ... find q = argmax a[i]*a[k-i]
                    # find j = argmin a[k-j] (maybe valid) not q
                    candidates.append(('pair', q, j))
            # line search for each candidate, evaluate best trial
            best_trial_m = cur_m
            best_move = None
            for cand in candidates:
                if considered too many? limit to e.g. 20 per block
                if fw:
                    L,B, bounds = compute_fw(a,c,j)
                    t_best, m_trial = line_search_generic(c,L,B,t_min=0,t_max=1, n_trial=24)
                    if m_trial < best_trial_m - 1e-18: best...
                if pair:
                    L,B, tmax = compute_pair(a,c,q,j)
                    # If s_q <= s_j + tol maybe skip? but peak-specific may.
                    if s_q > s_j: tmax positive; else maybe direction reverse.
                    t_best, m_trial = line_search_generic(...)
                    if m_trial < best_trial_m: best...
            if best_move is not None:
                apply move
                update cur_m = best_trial_m (or recompute max c after apply? We have trial m; update c exactly; but numerical max maybe recompute? Use max c after apply; cheap O(n) no FFT)
                if cur_m < best_m: best_m=cur_m; best_a=a.copy()
                accepted=True; update_count++
                no_improve_moves=0
                # optionally after each move, current c updated; s stale. If accepted many, break block to refresh? Could continue but selection stale. We can after each accepted maybe if update_count%1==0 break? To use fresh s.
                break? We'll break after one accepted to refresh? That would be slow if refresh every move. We can allow multiple candidates but once accepted, restart block (break) to recompute s if counter. Simpler: after accepted, continue outer while? We can set restart=True.
            else:
                no_improve_moves +=1; break block
        if no_improve_moves > say 5:
            # perturb or adjust tau/random, or restart with smoothed noise
            if update_count % 10 == 0:
                a_pert = add_noise(a, sigma=0.005 relative)
                c=conv_square(a_pert); cur_m=...
                if cur_m < best_m*1.01? accept? else continue perturbation.
            else:
                # change tau? not within function unless parameter.
```

But computing argsort(s) of size 6200 per block cheap. We can do once per block.

Line search generic:
```
def line_search_max(c, L, B, t_min, t_max, n_coarse=24, n_fine=24, eps=1e-18):
    best_t = t_min; best_val = np.max(c + t_min*L + t_min*t_min*B) # t_min zero: max(c)
    # If t_max <= t_min return
    # Coarse fractions: maybe include small fractions because optimum often small.
    fr = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n_coarse)
    # but if t_max large, large α may spike; include logspace: generations [1e-3,1e-2,...] plus linear.
    frs = np.unique(np.concatenate([np.linspace(0,1,20), np.logspace(-4,0,20) if t_max>0]))
    for f in frs:
       t = t_min + f*(t_max-t_min)
       val = max(c + t*L + t*t*B)
       if val < best_val: ...
    # refine around best_f: step = best_f/10? Use local golden? We'll do 30 steps scanning increments.
    if best_t >0:
       half = (t_max - best_t)*0.5? We don't know neighbors. Use step = (t_max)/(2*n_coarse) initially, shrink.
       improved=True
       while improved and steps<10:
          improved=False
          for delta in np.linspace(-step, step, 21):
             t=best_t+delta; clamp
             val=...
             if val<best_val-1e-18: best=...
             # also maybe choose best from coarse outside
          step*=0.5
    return best_t,best_val
```
This may be many max operations. We can optimize by using function `trial_max(t)`.
Because c,L,B arrays. For t_min=0, best_val initial cur_m (max c) may have noise. Use `np.max(c)` precomputed. For each trial, allocate new array `c_t = c + t*L + t2*B`; memory per trial. We can reduce allocations by computing in a preallocated array? But simpler okay; 40 trials*12k elements ~0.5M floats allocated 4MB; per heavy? Actually each allocation 100KB, 40=4MB, fine. For many candidates, maybe significant but OK. Could use `np.max(c + t*L + t2*B, out?)` no out. Python overhead.

But we can do cheaper initial screening using weighted linear approximation? For each candidate, before full line search, estimate best possible via weighted sum? Not necessary.

We can precompute `cur_max = np.max(c)` but after updates c changed; O(n) compute at block.

`compute_fw` arrays:
```
def build_fw_directions(a,c,j):
    L = -2.0*c  # length 2n-1
    S = np.zeros_like(c)
    r=j
    S[r:r+n] = a
    L += 2.0*S
    B = c - 2.0*S
    idx=2*j
    if 0<=idx<2*n-1: B[idx]+=1.0
    return L,B,1.0
```
Check formula: c + α(2S-2c)+α^2(c-2S+δ). Yes L=2S-2c; B=c-2S; B[2j]+=1.
For α=1, a=e_j, c should be δ_{2j}. Let's test: c(1)=c+2S-2c + c-2S+δ = δ. Good.

`build_pair_directions(a,c,q,j)`:
```
L = zeros; S_j, S_q
L = 2*(S_j - S_q)
B = zeros
B[2j]+=1; B[2q]+=1; B[j+q]-=2 (if q!=j)
tmax=a[q]
```
If moving from q to j. If s_q < s_j (i.e., direction would increase weighted sum), we can swap q,j and tmax=a[j]. We'll generate direction with source high, dest low.
For q,j same skip.
At t=1? If t=tmax=a_q, a_q=0. Formula exact.

`trial_max` could use B small; but if B full zeros, adding `t2*B` array each trial. We can optimize pair B: Store indices and coefficients, and for trial compute base = c + t*L; val=np.max(base); Then adjust 3 indices: for (idx,b) in list: val = max(val, base[idx]+t2*b). But `base[idx]` after full vector. This avoids B full addition. For many trials, B full addition cost length. Better:
- For FW, B = c - 2S + δ is dense (because c and S dense), so need full addition.
- For pair, B has at most 3 nonzeros! We can exploit: `base = c + t*L` where L=2(S_j-S_q) dense. Then val=np.max(base); then adjust indices: val = max(val, base[idx2j]+t2, base[idx2q]+t2, base[idxjq]-2t2). But max over modified coefficients may also decrease? Since some coefficients modified by t2, but val should be max of modified array. Starting val=max(base); modifying an existing coefficient could increase or decrease. If it decreases, max unchanged; if increases, val max. So yes just take max of base and modified values. Thus for pair line search, no B array needed, only base calculation and 3 point checks. For FW, B dense; cannot. But FW B = c -2S_j + δ = (c -2S_j) dense. So full.
Actually for pair, linear L also can be computed without full S_j/S_q? L[k]=2(a_{k-j}-a_{k-q}) dense; need full for max. OK.

For pair trial, `base = c + t*L`; `val = base.max();` adjust indices. Need ensure if modified index had max and b negative, true max may be lower than base.max(); using base.max() overestimates if the global max is at that modified index and modified downward. Example base max at idx with -2t^2, val after may be second max lower. Our `max(base, modified values)` gives base.max (unmodified value), overestimates objective. Need exact. For negative B at j+q, if base max there, true max decreases; overestimate may reject beneficial move. For boundary positive B, overestimate if base max? positive increases, exact needs modified. We need exact. We can compute full base then set indices to base[ idx ] + t2*b (loop 3), then max. That's still only modifying 3, no full B addition. Do:
```
arr = c + t*L
for idx,b in mods: arr[idx] += t2*b
val = arr.max()
```
This allocates and modifies few. Good.

Given p weights for s. Weighted sum W=∑p c. For direction d amount t: W(t)=W + t∑p L_k + t^2 ∑p B_k. For pair direction q->j: linear =2(s_j - s_q). Quadratic = p_{2j}+p_{2q}-2p_{j+q} (with p length). We can find α minimizing W(α): derivative 2(s_j-W)+2α(W-2s_j+p_{2j}). If quadratic coefficient A = W-2s_j+p_{2j}; if A>0, minimizer t=-(B)/(2A) with B=2(s_j-W). But note W≥c_max? Detailed algorithm:
```
def direct_descent(a_init, time_limit, tau=100.0, K=128, mode='mix', block_time=5.0, seed=1, verbose=False):
   a=norm_sum1(a_init)
   c=conv_square(a)
   cur_m=conv_max(c)  # max, maybe clip negative
   best_a=a.copy(); best_m=cur_m
   t0=...
   rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
   refresh_count=0
   no_improve=0
   total_moves=0
   while time_left:
       # Build weights if needed
       p, s, W = None
       if refresh_count==0 or no_improve>...:
           p = soft_weights(c,tau,K)
           s = gradient_from_weights(p,a)
           W = float(np.dot(p,c))
           refresh_count=0
       # find best move using function best_direct_move(a,c,s,p,W,tau,K,mode,cur_m, rng for perturbation maybe)
       move = find_best_move(a,c,s,p,W,cur_m,mode)
       if move is not None:
           apply_move
           cur_m = move.m  # or max(c)
           total_moves++
           if cur_m < best_m - 1e-18:
               best_m=cur_m; best_a=a.copy(); no_improve=0
           else:
               no_improve += 1  # current M may not improve but move reduced? We accepted only if cur_m<old_m so should improve current, but best maybe same because older? Actually old_m was current, if improved current, best should improve unless there was perturbation? If current was worse than best, moving down to best level not update. no_improve relative best.
           if total_moves % 3 ==0:
               s=None; refresh_count=-1
       else:
           no_improve += 1
           # after many no improve, perturb or change weights
           if no_improve > 8:
               # Try random restart/perturb
               sigma = 0.01 + 0.05*min(1, no_improve/20)
               a_new = perturb(a, sigma, rng)
               c_new = conv_square(a_new); m_new=...
               if m_new < cur_m:
                   accept; no_improve=0; best update
               elif m_new < best_m*1.05 and rng.random()<0.5:
                   accept current (escape) but no_improve=0? maybe set cur=m
               else:
                   # try smaller sigma or reset to best_a
                   if no_improve > 20:
                       a=best_a.copy(); c=conv_square(a); cur_m=best_m; no_improve=5; tau? can't change here
           else:
               # maybe refresh weights earlier
               s=None
       refresh_count +=1
   return best_a, c_for_best? .
```
But if function chooses same tau always, when stuck maybe outside schedule changes.

`evaluate_move`:
FW:
```
def exact_fw(c,a,j,ts,cur_m):
   n=len(a)
   L = -2*c.copy()
   S = np.zeros_like(c)
   S[j:j+n]=a
   L += 2*S
   B = c - 2*S
   if 2*j < len(c): B[2*j]+=1
   best_t=0; best_val=cur_m
   for t in ts:
      val = np.max(c + t*L + t*t*B)
      if val < best_val: ...
   # refine around best_t: maybe check 5 nearby
   return best_t, best_val, L,B
```
For pair:
```
L = np.zeros_like(c); S_j, S_q? To avoid two zero arrays:
   L = 2* (shifted a by j) - 2* (shifted a by q)
We can compute:
   L = np.zeros_like(c)
   L[j:j+n] += 2*a
   L[q:q+n] -= 2*a
mods=[(2j,1),(2q,1),(j+q,-2)]
for t:
   arr=c + t*L
   t2=t*t
   for idx,b in mods: arr[idx] += t2*b
   val=max(arr)
```

Direct gradient formula: s_i = ∑_{k in top} w_k a_{k-i}. For each k,w, valid i segments: i_start=max(0,k-n+1), i_end=min(n,k+1). For i in that segment, add w*a[k-i]. We can do:
```
s=np.zeros(n)
for idx,w in zip(top_idx,top_vals):
   i0=max(0, idx-n+1); i1=min(n, idx+1)
   if i0<i1:
      # partner indices = idx - np.arange(i0,i1)
      s[i0:i1] += w * a[idx - np.arange(i0,i1)]
```
This uses `np.arange` each iteration; overhead. Could precompute range. For K=128, okay. But inside many direct blocks, maybe 128*5000=640k loops, high. FFT simpler. We'll use FFT always to reduce Python loops. For n small maybe direct okay but not necessary.

```
def gen_candidate_t(lin, quad, tmax):
    if tmax <= 1e-15: return [0.0]
    ts=[0.0, tmax]
    if abs(quad) > 1e-14:
        t0 = -lin/(2*quad)
        if t0 > 1e-15 and t0 < tmax:
            ts.append(t0)
            ts.append(max(1e-15, t0*0.5))
            ts.append(min(tmax, t0*1.5))
    # fractions of tmax
    for f in (0.01,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,0.8):
       t=f*tmax
       if t>1e-15: ts.append(t)
    # unique
    ts=np.array(ts); ts=ts[(ts>-1e-15)&(ts<tmax+1e-15)]
    ts=np.clip(ts,0,tmax)
    # remove close duplicates
    if len(ts):
       ts_sorted=np.unique(np.round(ts, 12))? Use unique exact.
    return ts.tolist()
```
But if tmax small many duplicates. OK.

Candidate W trial evaluation:
```
def min_w_trial(ts, W, lin, quad):
   best_w = float('inf'); best_t=0
   for t in ts:
      w = W + lin*t + quad*t*t
      if w < best_w: best_w=w; best_t=t
   return best_w,best_t
```

Now, direct_descent loop more concretely:
```
def direct_descent(a_init, time_limit, tau=100, K=128, mode='mix', seed=0, verbose=False, block_refresh=1, max_no_improve=30):
    n=len(a)
    a=norm_sum1(a_init)
    c=conv_square(a)
    c=np.maximum(c,0.0) # true nonneg; but if max? OK
    cur_m=float(c.max())
    best_a=a.copy(); best_m=cur_m
    t0=time.monotonic()
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    no_improve=0
    move_count=0
    last_len? 
    s=None
    while time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit:
       # recompute weights if s None
       if s is None:
           p, top_idx, top_vals = soft_weights(c, tau, K)
           if K <= 128:
              s = gradient_from_top(top_idx, top_vals, a)
           else:
              s = gradient_from_weights_fft(p,a)
           W = float(np.dot(p,c))
           # If W very close to max, ok. Maybe p all zero? no.
       # Find best move
       move = find_best_move(a,c,s,p,W,cur_m,mode)
       if move is not None:
           # move tuple: (type_code,q,j,t,new_m)
           apply...
           # c max
           cur_m = float(np.max(c))
           if cur_m < best_m - 1e-18:
               best_m=cur_m; best_a=a.copy(); no_improve=0
           else:
               no_improve += 1
           move_count += 1
           # If many moves, reset s=None to recompute; maybe every 2
           if move_count % 2 == 0:
               s=None
           # if no_improve large but current improved below old? already.
       else:
           no_improve += 1
           s=None # attempt different weights? same tau though.
           if no_improve > 15:
               # perturb
               sigma = 0.005 + 0.01*min(1.0, no_improve/30.0)
               # Try several perturbations, accept if not much worse
               accepted=False
               for _ in range(5):
                   a_try = a * (1.0 + sigma*(rng.standard_normal(n)-0.5?))
                   # Use multiplicative noise to keep positive:
                   noise = np.exp(sigma*rng.normal(size=n))
                   a_try = a * noise
                   s_try = a_try.sum()
                   if s_try > 0:
                      a_try /= s_try
                   c_try = conv_square(a_try); c_try=np.maximum(c_try,0)
                   m_try = float(c_try.max())
                   if m_try < cur_m:
                       a=a_try; c=c_try; cur_m=m_try; accepted=True; no_improve=0
                       if m_try < best_m: best...
                       break
                   elif m_try < best_m * 1.03 and rng.random()<0.3:
                       # escape
                       a=a_try; c=c_try; cur_m=m_try; accepted=True; no_improve=0; break
               if not accepted and no_improve > 40:
                   # reset to best
                   a=best_a.copy(); c=conv_square(a); c=np.maximum(c,0); cur_m=best_m; no_improve=10
           if no_improve > 60:
               # maybe break block, let outside schedule change params
               break
    return best_a, best_m
```

```
def initial_candidates(n_base):
   x=np.linspace(0,1,n_base)
   seqs = []
   labels = []
   if parent: seqs.append(norm_sum1(parent)); labels.append('parent')
```

`gradient_from_weights_fft(p,a)`:
```
def gradient_from_weights_fft(p, a):
    n = len(a)
    m = len(p)
    L = 1 << (n + m - 2).bit_length()  # since max conv index n-1 + m-1 = n+m-2, length n+m-1, size-1 = n+m-2
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    P = np.fft.rfft(p[::-1], L)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(A * P, L)
    idx = 2*n - 2 - np.arange(n)
    return conv[idx]
```
Check L: conv length n+m-1, last index n+m-2. Need L > n+m-2. `1 << (n + m - 2).bit_length()` works if n+m-2>0. For n=1,m=1, n+m-2=0, bit_length 0, 1<<0=1. OK? But rfft length 1 handles. `idx = 0 - arange(1) = [0]`.
If using p length 2n-1, n+m-2=3n-3, bit_length. For n=6200, 18597 bit_length 15? 2^14=16384 <18597, 2^15=32768. Good.

`eval_fw_move`:
```
def eval_fw_move(c,a,j,ts,cur_m):
    n = len(a); m=2*n-1
    best_t=0.0; best_val=cur_m
    # precompute S only once
    S = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
    if n > 0:
        S[j:j+n] = a
    L = 2.0*S - 2.0*c
    B = c - 2.0*S
    idx2 = 2*j
    if 0 <= idx2 < m: B[idx2] += 1.0
    for t in ts:
       if t <= 1e-15:
          val = cur_m
       else:
          arr = c + t*L + t*t*B
          val = float(np.max(arr))
       if val < best_val - 1e-18:
          best_val=val; best_t=t
    # refine around best_t
    if best_t > 0 and best_t < 1:
       step = max(1e-5, best_t*0.1)
       for _ in range(3):
          improved=False
          for f in (-1.0, -0.5, 0.5, 1.0):
              t = best_t + f*step
              if t < 0 or t > 1: continue
              arr = c + t*L + t*t*B
              val=float(np.max(arr))
              if val < best_val - 1e-17:
                  best_val=val; best_t=t; improved=True
          if not improved: break
          step *= 0.4
    return best_t,best_val
```
But for t=0, val should be max(c) maybe cur_m. If c had negative and cur_m stale, use max(c). Pass cur_m = max(c). Good.
Allocating arr each trial; okay. Could if t=0 not allocate.
For refine, if best_t=0 no refine. Combining arrays maybe memory. Fine.

```
def apply_pair_move(a,c,q,j,t):
    n=len(a); m=2*n-1
    if t <=1e-15: return a,c,float(np.max(c))
    L = np.zeros(m)
    L[j:j+n] += 2.0*a
    L[q:q+n] -= 2.0*a
    t2=t*t
    c = c + t*L  # creates new
    c[2*j] += t2
    c[2*q] += t2
    c[j+q] -= 2*t2
    a = a.copy()? If update in place:
       a[j] += t; a[q] -= t
    # clip
    a[q] = max(0,a[q]); a[j]=max(0,a[j])
    sa=a.sum(); if abs(sa-1)>1e-12: a/=sa; c/=sa*sa
    return a,c,float(np.max(c))
```

Perturbation top-level:
```
def perturb_sequence(a, rng, sigma=0.02):
   n=len(a)
   # multiplicative lognormal
   noise = np.exp(sigma*rng.normal(size=n))
   a_try = a*noise
   # Also try adding small uniform noise to low coordinates?
   a_try = np.maximum(a_try, 0.0)
   s=a_try.sum()
   if s < 1e-15: return a.copy()
   return a_try/s
```

`find_best_move`:
```
def find_best_move(a,c,s,p,W,cur_m,mode):
    n=len(a)
    if n <= 1: return None
    m=2*n-1
    # If s has NaN? set zero.
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(s)): s=np.nan_to_num(s)
    # low/high counts adapt n
    low_cnt = min(8, n)
    high_cnt = min(8, n)
    low_idx = np.argsort(s, kind='stable')[:low_cnt]
    high_idx = np.argsort(s, kind='stable')[::-1][:high_cnt]
    # candidates list
    cand = []
    W_safe = W if np.isfinite(W) else cur_m
    if mode in ('fw','mix'):
       # include low_idx plus endpoints maybe
       chars = list(low_idx)
       # add 0,n-1 if not present
       if 0 not in chars: chars.append(0)
       if n-1 not in chars: chars.append(n-1)
       for j in chars[:10]:
           idx2=2*j
           pval = p[idx2] if 0 <= idx2 < m else 0.0
           lin = 2.0*(s[j] - W_safe)
           quad = W_safe - 2.0*s[j] + pval
           ts = gen_candidate_ts(lin, quad, 1.0)
           if len(ts)>0:
              wmin = min_scalar_quad(W_safe, lin, quad, ts)
              cand.append((wmin, 0, 0, j, ts))
    if mode in ('pair','mix'):
       # limit pairs
       low_pairs = low_idx[:5]
       high_pairs = high_idx[:5]
       for q in high_pairs:
          if a[q] <= 1e-15: continue
          for j in low_pairs:
             if j == q: continue
             lin = 2.0*(s[j] - s[q])
             if lin > 1e-12:
                 # still may if peak-specific, skip generic
                 continue
             idxj=2*j; idxq=2*q; idxs=j+q
             quad = (p[idxj] if idxj<m else 0) + (p[idxq] if idxq<m else 0) - 2*(p[idxs] if idxs<m else 0)
             tmax = a[q]
             ts = gen_candidate_ts(lin, quad, tmax)
             if len(ts)>0:
                wmin = min_scalar_quad(W_safe, lin, quad, ts)
                cand.append((wmin, 1, q, j, ts))
       # peak-specific
       top_peak_cnt = min(3, m)
       # get top peak indices using argpartition
       if top_peak_cnt < m:
          kpart = np.argpartition(c, m-top_peak_cnt)[-(top_peak_cnt):]
       else: kpart=np.arange(m)
       for k in kpart:
          # compute partner array for peak k and source/dest
          i_start = max(0, int(k)-n+1)
          i_end = min(n, int(k)+1)
          if i_start >= i_end: continue
          ii = np.arange(i_start, i_end)
          vals = a[ii] * a[int(k)-ii]
          maxval = float(np.max(vals))
          if maxval <= 1e-30: continue
          q = int(ii[np.argmax(vals)])
          # partner for all coordinates
          partner = np.zeros(n)
          # for j valid range: j_start=max(0,k-n+1), j_end=min(n,k+1); partner[j]=a[k-j]
          j_start=max(0, int(k)-n+1); j_end=min(n,int(k)+1)
          jj=np.arange(j_start,j_end)
          partner[jj]=a[int(k)-jj]
          # choose dest: min partner not q. If partner min is q, choose second.
          # To handle many equal min, prefer low s if possible.
          # simplest: order by partner, then s
          # can't use stable argsort with key; use idx = np.argsort(partner); choose first !=q
          idx_ord = np.argsort(partner, kind='stable')
          j = int(idx_ord[0])
          if j == q:
             if len(idx_ord)>1: j=int(idx_ord[1])
             else: continue
          # If s_j > s_q maybe, still evaluate
          lin = 2.0*(partner[j] - partner[q])  # for this peak; but W approximate uses s? Use s difference for global W.
          # For scalar W approx, use s[j]-s[q]
          linW = 2.0*(s[j]-s[q])
          idxj=2*j; idxq=2*q; idxs=j+q
          quad = p[idxj] + p[idxq] - 2*p[idxs]
          tmax=a[q]
          ts=gen_candidate_ts(linW, quad, tmax)
          if len(ts)>0:
             wmin=min_scalar_quad(W_safe, linW, quad, ts)
             cand.append((wmin,1,q,j,ts))
    if not cand:
       # fallback: FW to lowest s
       if n>0:
          j=int(np.argmin(s))
          idx2=2*j
          lin=2*(s[j]-W_safe); quad=W_safe-2*s[j]+(p[idx2] if 2*j<m else 0)
          ts=[0.0,0.05,0.2,0.5]
          wmin=...
          cand=[(wmin,0,0,j,ts)]
          if not cand: return None
    cand.sort(key=candidate_key)
    eval_top = 10
    best_info=None; best_mval=cur_m
    for entry in cand[:eval_top]:
       wmin, typ, q, j, ts = entry
       if typ == 0:
          # optional if W min >= cur_m? still evaluate
          t_best, m_best = eval_fw_move(c,a,j,ts,cur_m)
       else:
          tmax = a[q]
          t_best, m_best = eval_pair_move(c,a,q,j,ts,cur_m,tmax)
       if m_best < best_mval - 1e-16:
          best_mval=m_best; best_info=(typ,q,j,t_best)
    if best_info is None:
       return None
    return (best_info[0], best_info[1], best_info[2], best_info[3], best_mval)
```
Need `min_scalar_quad` helper top-level.
`gen_candidate_ts` returns list; if tmax=1 for FW, includes 1.0. For FW, t=1 may create extreme e_j, likely bad but included.
`min_scalar_quad`:
```
def min_scalar_quad(W,lin,quad,ts):
    best=inf; 
    for t in ts:
       val = W + lin*t + quad*t*t
       if val<best: best=val
    return best
```
Need handle `inf` if ts empty.

Potential over-evaluation: For each candidate, ts length maybe up to 10; exact eval loops each and allocates. If 10 candidates *10=100 arrays length 12k =>1.2M ops. Good.

However, `eval_fw_move` recomputes S,L,B for ts; it precomputes once. Good. For top 10 candidates, 10 dense builds. OK.

Refinement inside eval for each candidate may add arrays. Could be heavy. We can only refine if initial best_val < cur_m and candidate is among top? But eval doesn't know. We can reduce refine to 1 loop if t in interior. Maybe 3 loops *4 offsets=12 extra arrays per eval. 100 extra arrays, okay? 100*12k=1.2M additional. Fine. But if 100 candidates? limited 10. Good.

`gen_candidate_ts` unique: Use Python list then `np.unique`. To avoid sorting? We can use `np.unique(np.array(ts))`. For pair tmax small, duplicates. If tmax=0, returns [0]. Need if ts contains tiny negative due t0? clip.
```
def gen_candidate_ts(lin, quad, tmax):
   if tmax <= 1e-15: return [0.0]
   ts=[0.0, float(tmax)]
   if np.isfinite(lin) and np.isfinite(quad):
      if abs(quad) > 1e-14:
         t0 = -lin/(2.0*quad)
         if np.isfinite(t0):
            if t0 > 1e-15: ts.append(t0)
            if t0 > 1e-15: ts.append(t0*0.5); ts.append(t0*1.5)
      elif abs(lin) > 1e-15:
         if lin > 0: ts.append(0.0)
         else: ts.append(tmax)
   for f in (0.01,0.03,0.1,0.3,0.6,0.9):
       t=f*tmax
       if t>1e-15: ts.append(t)
   arr=np.array(ts,dtype=float)
   arr=arr[(arr>=-1e-15)&(arr<=tmax+1e-15)]
   np.clip(arr,0,tmax,out=arr)
   if len(arr)==0: return [0.0]
   arr=np.unique(arr)
   # limit to 12
   if len(arr)>12:
       # keep 0,tmax, and fractions? simplify
       keep=[0.0,tmax]
       for f in (0.01,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,0.8): keep.append(f*tmax)
       arr=np.array(keep)
       arr=np.unique(arr)
   return [float(x) for x in arr]
```
This ensures limited.

```
    return item[0]
```

Now, `direct_descent` more exact:
```
def direct_descent(a_init, time_limit, tau=100.0, K=128, mode='mix', seed=0, early_stop=30.0, verbose=True, recompute_every=3):
   n=len(a_init)
   if n <=1: return a_init copy, max(conv_square)
   a=norm_sum1(a_init)
   c=conv_square(a); c=np.maximum(c,0)
   cur_m=float(c.max())
   best_a=a.copy(); best_m=cur_m
   initial_m=best_m
   t0=time.monotonic()
   last_improve=t0
   rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
   s=None; p=None
   move_count=0; no_improve=0
   while time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit:
      elapsed=time.monotonic()-t0
      if early_stop > 0 and elapsed > early_stop and best_m >= initial_m - 1e-12 and last_improve < t0 + 5:
          # no improvement since start? break
          break
      if s is None:
         p, top_idx, top_vals = soft_weights(c, tau, K)
         if K <= 128:
            s = gradient_from_top(top_idx, top_vals, a)
         else:
            s = gradient_from_weights_fft(p, a)
         W = float(np.dot(p,c))
         if not np.isfinite(W): W=cur_m
      move = find_best_move(a,c,s,p,W,cur_m,mode)
      if move is not None:
         typ,q,j,t,new_m = move
         if typ == 0:
            a,c,cur_m = apply_fw_move(a,c,j,t)
         else:
            a,c,cur_m = apply_pair_move(a,c,q,j,t)
         if cur_m < best_m - 1e-18:
            best_m=cur_m; best_a=a.copy(); last_improve=time.monotonic(); no_improve=0
         else:
            no_improve += 1
         move_count +=1
         if move_count % recompute_every == 0:
            # recompute c to remove drift and refresh
            a=norm_sum1(a); c=conv_square(a); c=np.maximum(c,0); cur_m=float(c.max())
            if cur_m < best_m - 1e-18: update...
            s=None
         # optional sym after 2 moves if no improve? maybe after each if no improve
         if no_improve > 0 and move_count % 5 == 0:
             sym = try_symmetrize(a,c,cur_m)
             if sym is not None:
                a,c,cur_m=sym
                if cur_m < best_m: update...
                s=None
      else:
         no_improve += 1
         s=None
         if no_improve > 5:
             # try symmetrize
             sym=try_symmetrize(a,c,cur_m)
             if sym is not None: ...; no_improve=0
             else:
                # perturb
                found=False
                sig_base = 0.005 + 0.02*min(1.0, no_improve/30.0)
                for _ in range(4):
                    sigma = sig_base * (0.5 + rng.random())
                    a_try = perturb_sequence(a, rng, sigma)
                    c_try=conv_square(a_try); c_try=np.maximum(c_try,0)
                    m_try=float(c_try.max())
                    if m_try < cur_m - 1e-15:
                       a=a_try; c=c_try; cur_m=m_try; no_improve=0; s=None; found=True
                       if m_try<best_m: update; last_improve
                       break
                    elif m_try < best_m * 1.02 and rng.random() < 0.4:
                       a=a_try; c=c_try; cur_m=m_try; no_improve=0; s=None; found=True
                       break
                if not found and no_improve > 25:
                    # reset to best and break? maybe change param outside
                    a=best_a.copy(); c=conv_square(a); c=np.maximum(c,0); cur_m=best_m; no_improve=10
         if no_improve > 40:
             break
      if best_m < initial_m - 1e-12:
          # progress; reset no_improve? already. Maybe update early stop limit.
          pass
   if verbose:
      print(...)
   return best_a, best_m
```
Need ensure when recomputing c after moves, if cur_m updates best? Yes.
When no move, `s=None` each time causing recompute weights same c; expensive if repeated. We should not recompute same until perturb. Better: if no move, don't set s=None immediately; just increment no_improve. If no_improve reaches threshold, then recompute with maybe different? Since tau fixed, same weights likely same no move. We can break early. So set s=None only after perturb/sym. In no move loop:
```
move = find_best_move(...)
if move None:
  no_improve +=1
  if no_improve == 6: try sym/perturb; s=None
  elif no_improve > 30: break
```
Don't recompute each. For move accepted, recompute every few moves.

Also `find_best_move` can return None if all candidate exact > cur_m. If no move with same weights, repeating won't help. So no_move_no_recompute until perturb.

Early stop: `early_stop` should be time since last best improvement. Use:
```
if best_m < initial_m - 1e-12:
    last_improve updated
if time.monotonic()-last_improve > early_stop: break
```
But if initial best already global best and no improvement, break after early_stop. Good. If we start from perturbed worse than best, best_m is global best at start (initial_m=best_m). Improving current but not best shouldn't update last_improve? We want allow time to recover perhaps, but if current worse due outside, not. We always start from best_seq, so current=best. OK.

Set `last_improve` when best_m improves. If perturbation leads to new best later, update. Good.

Use `recompute_every=2` maybe. If too frequent, FFT. But direct moves analytic; after 2 moves recompute ensures accuracy. We'll set 3.

Now, safe in `direct_descent`: If n large and time_limit small, initial conv_square and gradient heavy. But ok.

Let's consider performance of `conv_square` called in perturb attempts: each attempt maybe 4 per no_improve; if stuck, many. But break after 40 no_improve. Could be 10 convs, okay. Maybe reduce perturb attempts to 3.

Need top-level `make_arcsine`:
```
def make_arcsine(n, eps):
    x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    denom=np.sqrt(x*(1-x)+eps)
    a=1.0/denom
    return norm_sum1(np.maximum(a,0))
```

```
a=np.exp(-x/(width)) + np.exp(-(1-x)/width) + 0.01
```
This high endpoints, not center. `make_random_smooth` top-level as above.

Norm sum: if sum < 1e-15 return ones/n.

Detailed function implementations:

```
def norm_sum1(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.ndim != 1:
        a = a.ravel()
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    n = a.size
    if n == 0 or s < 1e-300:
        return np.ones(max(1,n), dtype=np.float64) / max(1,n)
    a = a / s
    return a
```
If n=0, returns length1. But our sequences nonempty.

`conv_square`:
```
def conv_square(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0: return np.array([0.0])
    size = 2*n - 1
    fft_size = 1 << (size - 1).bit_length()
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, fft_size)
    C = np.fft.irfft(A * A, fft_size)[:size]
    # remove tiny imaginary? irfft real
    return C
```
If n=1, size=1, fft_size=1, rfft length1 works? np.fft.rfft with n=1 returns shape1. irfft with n=1? `np.fft.irfft([A0], 1)` works. Good.

`safescore` uses list; if a length huge, list comp. OK.

```
def make_u_shape(n, alpha):
    x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    a=1.0 + alpha*(x-0.5)**2
    return norm_sum1(a)
```
`make_arcsine`:
```
def make_arcsine(n, eps):
    x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    a=1.0/np.sqrt(x*(1.0-x)+eps)
    a[0] = max(a[0], 0.0) # no need
    return norm_sum1(a)
```
Endpoint finite. `make_bimodal`:

```
def make_bimodal(n, width):
    x=...
    a=np.exp(-x/width)+np.exp(-(1-x)/width)+0.01
    return norm_sum1(a)
```
`make_random_smooth`:
```
def make_random_smooth(n, seed, modes=30):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    a=np.ones(n)*0.5
    for f in range(1, modes+1):
       amp = 0.8/(f**0.3)
       a += amp*rng.normal(0.0,0.4)*np.cos(2*np.pi*f*x + rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi))
    a=np.maximum(a,1e-4)
    return norm_sum1(a)
```
Maybe alpha too large causing negative? clip.

`interpolate_sequence`:
```
def interpolate_sequence(a, new_n):
    a=norm_sum1(a)
    old_n=a.size
    if old_n == new_n: return a.copy()
    if new_n <=0: return a.copy()
    old_x=np.linspace(0,1,old_n)
    new_x=np.linspace(0,1,new_n)
    b=np.interp(new_x, old_x, a)
    b=np.maximum(b,0.0)
    s=b.sum()
    if s<1e-300: b=np.ones(new_n)/new_n
    else: b=b/s
    return b
```

`soft_weights`:
```
def soft_weights(c, tau, K):
    c=np.asarray(c,float)
    m=c.size
    if m==0: ...
    if tau <= 0:
       weights=np.zeros(m)
       if K >= m:
          w=1.0/m
          weights.fill(w)
          return weights, np.arange(m), weights.copy()
       else:
          K=min(K,m)
          idx=np.argpartition(c, m-K)[-(K):]
          weights[idx]=1.0/K
          return weights, idx, weights[idx].copy()
    if K >= m:
       idx_all=np.arange(m)
       vals=c
    else:
       K=min(K,m)
       idx_all=np.argpartition(c, m-K)[-(K):]
       vals=c[idx_all]
    vmax=float(np.max(vals))
    if not np.isfinite(vmax): vmax=0.0
    scaled=np.exp(tau*(vals-vmax))
    s=float(np.sum(scaled))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
       scaled=np.ones_like(vals)/vals.size
       s=scaled.sum()
    w=scaled
    weights=np.zeros(m)
    weights[idx_all]=w
    return weights, idx_all, w
```
Need if K=0? Ensure K=max(1,int(K)).

`gradient_from_top`:
```
def gradient_from_top(top_idx, top_vals, a):
    n=a.size
    s=np.zeros(n)
    if len(top_idx)==0: return s
    mask = top_vals > 1e-300
    idx = top_idx[mask]
    vals = top_vals[mask]
    for t in range(len(idx)):
       k=int(idx[t]); w=float(vals[t])
       if w == 0: continue
       i0 = k - n + 1
       if i0 < 0: i0 = 0
       i1 = k + 1
       if i1 > n: i1 = n
       if i0 < i1:
           # slice a[k-i1+1 : k-i0+1]
           start = k - i1 + 1
           stop = k - i0 + 1
           seg = a[start:stop][::-1]
           s[i0:i1] += w * seg
    return s
```
Check start>=0 and stop<=n. For k=0,i1=1,start0 stop1. For k=2n-2,i0=n-1,i1=n,start=k-n+1=n-1, stop=k-(n-1)+1=2n-2-n+2? = n? use a[n-1:n]. OK.

`gradient_from_weights_fft` as above.

`gen_candidate_ts`: Ensure tmax finite. Use `np.isfinite`.

```
def gen_candidate_ts(lin, quad, tmax):
    if not np.isfinite(tmax) or tmax <= 1e-15:
        return [0.0]
    ts=[0.0, float(tmax)]
    if np.isfinite(lin) and np.isfinite(quad):
       if abs(quad) > 1e-14:
          t0 = -lin/(2.0*quad)
          if np.isfinite(t0):
             if t0 > 1e-15:
                ts.append(float(t0))
                ts.append(float(0.5*t0))
                ts.append(float(1.5*t0))
       else:
          if lin < 0:
             ts.append(float(tmax))
          else:
             ts.append(0.0)
    for f in (0.01,0.03,0.1,0.3,0.6,0.9):
       t=f*tmax
       if 1e-15 < t < tmax-1e-15:
          ts.append(float(t))
    arr=np.array(ts,dtype=np.float64)
    arr=arr[(arr >= -1e-15) & (arr <= tmax + 1e-15)]
    if arr.size == 0:
        return [0.0]
    np.clip(arr, 0.0, tmax, out=arr)
    arr=np.unique(arr)
    if arr.size > 12:
       keep = [0.0, tmax]
       for f in (0.01,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,0.8):
           keep.append(f*tmax)
       arr=np.unique(np.array(keep,dtype=np.float64))
    return [float(x) for x in arr]
```

`min_scalar_quad`:
```
def min_scalar_quad(W, lin, quad, ts):
    best = float('inf')
    for t in ts:
       val = W + lin*t + quad*t*t
       if np.isfinite(val) and val < best:
          best=val
    return best
```

`candidate_key`.

`eval_fw_move`:
Need if `j` out of range? no. Use `tmax`? For FW tmax=1. Could pass tmax for refine. We'll pass `tmax=1.0` default.
```
def eval_fw_move(c,a,j,ts,cur_m,tmax=1.0):
    n=a.size; m=c.size
    best_t=0.0; best_val=cur_m
    S=np.zeros(m)
    S[j:j+n] = a
    L=2.0*S - 2.0*c
    B=c - 2.0*S
    idx2=2*j
    if 0 <= idx2 < m: B[idx2] += 1.0
    max_t = tmax
    for t in ts:
       if t <= 1e-15:
          val = cur_m
       else:
          tt=float(t)
          if tt > max_t: tt=max_t
          arr = c + tt*L + tt*tt*B
          val = float(np.max(arr))
       if val < best_val - 1e-17:
          best_val=val; best_t=float(tt)
    if 0.0 < best_t < max_t:
       step = max(1e-6, 0.05*best_t)
       step = min(step, max_t/3.0)
       for _ in range(3):
          improved=False
          for f in (-1.0,-0.5,0.5,1.0):
              t=best_t + f*step
              if t < 0: t=0.0
              if t > max_t: t=max_t
              if t <= 1e-15: val=cur_m
              else: val=float(np.max(c + t*L + t*t*B))
              if val < best_val - 1e-17:
                  best_val=val; best_t=t; improved=True
          if not improved: break
          step *= 0.4
    return best_t,best_val
```
When computing arr multiple times, `c + ...` alloc. Good.
Potential issue: If cur_m not exact max(c) due c with negative, val for t=0 should be max(c). Pass cur_m. OK.

`apply_fw_move`:
```
def apply_fw_move(a,c,j,t):
    n=a.size; m=c.size
    t=max(0.0,min(1.0,float(t)))
    if t <= 1e-15:
       return a,c,float(np.max(c))
    S=np.zeros(m); S[j:j+n]=a
    L=2.0*S - 2.0*c
    B=c - 2.0*S
    idx2=2*j
    if 0<=idx2<m: B[idx2]+=1.0
    t2=t*t
    c = c + t*L + t2*B
    a = a * (1.0 - t)
    a[j] += t
    a = np.maximum(a,0.0)
    sa=float(a.sum())
    if sa < 1e-300:
       a = np.ones(n)/n; c = conv_square(a)
    elif abs(sa - 1.0) > 1e-12:
       a = a / sa; c = c / (sa*sa)
    # c maybe tiny negatives
    # don't clip every time to save? Could `c = np.where(c > 0, c, 0.0)` but this changes max if negative small. We can not.
    cur_m = float(np.max(c))
    if cur_m < 0: cur_m=0.0
    return a,c,cur_m
```
If t=1, a=all zero then a[j]=1, sa=1. c formula should δ. Good.

`apply_pair_move`:
```
def apply_pair_move(a,c,q,j,t):
   n=a.size; m=c.size
   if q==j: return a,c,float(np.max(c))
   tmax=float(a[q])
   t=max(0.0,min(tmax,float(t)))
   if t <=1e-15: return a,c,float(np.max(c))
   L=np.zeros(m)
   L[j:j+n] += 2.0*a
   L[q:q+n] -= 2.0*a
   t2=t*t
   c = c + t*L
   idxs=(2*j,2*q,j+q)
   coefs=(1.0,1.0,-2.0)
   # combine? Duplicates only if q=j; skip. But if 2j==j+q? q=j. Not.
   for idx,coef in ((2*j,1.0),(2*q,1.0),(j+q,-2.0)):
       if 0 <= idx < m:
          c[idx] += coef*t2
   a = a.copy()
   a[j] += t
   a[q] -= t
   a = np.maximum(a,0.0)
   sa=float(a.sum())
   if sa < 1e-300: ...
   elif abs(sa-1)>1e-12: a/=sa; c/=sa*sa
   cur_m=max(c)
   return
```
Using `a.copy()` avoids modifying original? We can update in place after L built. But if caller expects old a? We return new. Copy okay. But in direct, we assign a,c,m = apply. Good.
Maybe for FW we also copy? `a = a * (1-t)` creates new.

`try_symmetrize`:
```
def try_symmetrize(a,c,cur_m):
    n=a.size
    if n <= 1: return None
    a_sym = 0.5*(a + a[::-1])
    a_sym = np.maximum(a_sym,0.0)
    s=float(a_sym.sum())
    if s < 1e-15: return None
    a_sym = a_sym/s
    c_sym = conv_square(a_sym)
    c_sym = np.maximum(c_sym,0.0)
    m_sym = float(np.max(c_sym))
    if m_sym < cur_m - 1e-18:
       return a_sym,c_sym,m_sym
    return None
```

`perturb_sequence`:
```
def perturb_sequence(a, rng, sigma):
    n=a.size
    if n <=1: return a.copy()
    noise = np.exp(sigma * rng.normal(size=n))
    # Occasionally also add small uniform noise to low coords:
    a_try = a * noise
    # To increase exploration, set a few small coords to small positive?
    a_try = np.maximum(a_try, 0.0)
    s=float(a_try.sum())
    if s < 1e-15: return a.copy()
    a_try = a_try/s
    return a_try
```
Maybe multiplicative only doesn't create zeros; okay.

`direct_descent` implementation: Need careful with local variables and no nested functions.

Pseudo code with updates:
```
def direct_descent(a_init, time_limit, tau=100.0, K=128, mode='mix', seed=0, early_stop=30.0, verbose=False, recompute_every=3):
    a=norm_sum1(a_init)
    n=a.size
    if n <= 1:
       c=conv_square(a); m=float(np.max(np.maximum(c,0))); return a,m
    c=conv_square(a); c=np.maximum(c,0.0); cur_m=float(np.max(c))
    best_a=a.copy(); best_m=cur_m
    t0=time.monotonic(); last_improve=t0
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    s=None; p=None; W=None
    move_count=0; stall=0
    while time.monotonic() - t0 < time_limit:
        if early_stop > 0 and time.monotonic() - last_improve > early_stop:
            break
        if s is None:
            p, top_idx, top_vals = soft_weights(c, tau, K)
            if K <= 128:
                s = gradient_from_top(top_idx, top_vals, a)
            else:
                s = gradient_from_weights_fft(p,a)
            W = float(np.dot(p,c))
            if not np.isfinite(W): W=cur_m
        move = find_best_move(a,c,s,p,W,cur_m,mode)
        if move is not None:
            typ,q,j,t,new_m = move
            if typ == 0:
                a,c,cur_m = apply_fw_move(a,c,j,t)
            else:
                a,c,cur_m = apply_pair_move(a,c,q,j,t)
            if cur_m < best_m - 1e-18:
                best_m=cur_m; best_a=a.copy(); last_improve=time.monotonic(); stall=0
            else:
                stall += 1
            move_count += 1
            if move_count % recompute_every == 0:
                a=norm_sum1(a)
                c=conv_square(a); c=np.maximum(c,0.0); cur_m=float(np.max(c))
                if cur_m < best_m - 1e-18:
                    best_m=cur_m; best_a=a.copy(); last_improve=time.monotonic(); stall=0
                s=None
            if stall >= 8:
                # reset to best to avoid wandering
                a=best_a.copy(); c=conv_square(a); c=np.maximum(c,0.0); cur_m=float(np.max(c))
                best_m=cur_m; best_a=a.copy(); stall=0; s=None
        else:
            # no move: try escapes
            escaped=False
            sym = try_symmetrize(a,c,cur_m)
            if sym is not None:
                a,c,cur_m=sym
                if cur_m < best_m - 1e-18:
                    best_m=cur_m; best_a=a.copy(); last_improve=time.monotonic(); stall=0
                else:
                    # current improved but best same
                    pass
                escaped=True
                s=None
            if not escaped:
                base_sigma = 0.005 + 0.02*min(1.0, float(stall)/20.0)
                for _ in range(4):
                    sigma = base_sigma*(0.5 + rng.random())
                    a_try = perturb_sequence(a, rng, sigma)
                    c_try = conv_square(a_try); c_try=np.maximum(c_try,0.0)
                    m_try=float(np.max(c_try))
                    if m_try < cur_m - 1e-15:
                        a=a_try; c=c_try; cur_m=m_try
                        if m_try < best_m - 1e-18:
                            best_m=m_try; best_a=a.copy(); last_improve=time.monotonic(); stall=0
                        else:
                            stall=0
                        escaped=True
                        s=None
                        break
                    elif m_try < best_m * 1.02 and rng.random() < 0.3:
                        a=a_try; c=c_try; cur_m=m_try
                        stall=0
                        escaped=True
                        s=None
                        break
            if not escaped:
                if cur_m > best_m + 1e-12:
                    a=best_a.copy(); c=conv_square(a); c=np.maximum(c,0.0); cur_m=float(np.max(c))
                stall += 1
                if stall > 10:
                    break
            # if escaped, continue loop (s None)
    if verbose:
        print(f"  direct finish: n={n} m={best_m:.10f} est={2*n*best_m:.6f}")
    return best_a, best_m
```
Need be careful: In no move escape, if perturbation improves current but not best and cur_m > best_m, we accept current as worse to explore; but early_stop based on last_improve (best) will still run up to early_stop. If after accepting worse no best, time may expire. OK. But next loop if no move, stall reset could allow many perturbations. Could consume time; early_stop limits. Maybe okay.

If accepted move but best not improved (cur_m > best_m), stall increments. If stall>=8 reset to best. Good.

Potential bug: If `move` returns t=0? It shouldn't due m_best < cur. But maybe due tolerance. Apply with t small.

Now, `find_best_move` uses p, s, W. If p is None? no. Need if n large and `mode` string. Use `if mode == 'fw' or mode == 'mix'` to avoid `in`? `in` fine.

Let's write `find_best_move` without lambdas and no nested.

```
def find_best_move(a,c,s,p,W,cur_m,mode):
    n = a.size
    if n <= 1:
        return None
    m = 2*n - 1
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(s)):
        s = np.nan_to_num(s, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    if not np.isfinite(W):
        W = cur_m
    low_cnt = 8 if n > 8 else n
    high_cnt = 8 if n > 8 else n
    low_idx = np.argsort(s, kind='stable')[:low_cnt]
    high_idx = np.argsort(s, kind='stable')[::-1][:high_cnt]
    cand = []
    if mode == 'fw' or mode == 'mix':
        chars = [int(x) for x in low_idx]
        if 0 not in chars:
            chars.append(0)
        if n-1 not in chars:
            chars.append(n-1)
        for j in chars[:10]:
            idx2 = 2*j
            pidx = float(p[idx2]) if 0 <= idx2 < m else 0.0
            lin = 2.0*(float(s[j]) - W)
            quad = W - 2.0*float(s[j]) + pidx
            ts = gen_candidate_ts(lin, quad, 1.0)
            if len(ts) > 0:
                wmin = min_scalar_quad(W, lin, quad, ts)
                cand.append((wmin, 0, 0, j, ts))
    if mode == 'pair' or mode == 'mix':
        low_pairs = low_idx[:5]
        high_pairs = high_idx[:5]
        for qq in high_pairs:
            q=int(qq)
            if a[q] <= 1e-15: continue
            for jj in low_pairs:
                j=int(jj)
                if j == q: continue
                lin = 2.0*(float(s[j]) - float(s[q]))
                if lin > 1e-12:
                    continue
                idxj=2*j; idxq=2*q; idxs=j+q
                quad = (float(p[idxj]) if idxj<m else 0.0) + (float(p[idxq]) if idxq<m else 0.0) - 2.0*(float(p[idxs]) if idxs<m else 0.0)
                tmax=float(a[q])
                if tmax <= 1e-15: continue
                ts=gen_candidate_ts(lin, quad, tmax)
                if len(ts)>0:
                   wmin=min_scalar_quad(W, lin, quad, ts)
                   cand.append((wmin,1,q,j,ts))
        # peaks
        top_peak_cnt = 3 if m > 3 else m
        if top_peak_cnt < m:
            kpart = np.argpartition(c, m - top_peak_cnt)[-(top_peak_cnt):]
        else:
            kpart = np.arange(m)
        for kk in kpart:
            k=int(kk)
            i_start = max(0, k - n + 1)
            i_end = min(n, k + 1)
            if i_start >= i_end: continue
            ii = np.arange(i_start, i_end)
            vals = a[ii] * a[k - ii]
            maxval = float(np.max(vals))
            if maxval <= 1e-30: continue
            argmax_i = int(np.argmax(vals))
            q = int(ii[argmax_i])
            partner = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
            j_start = max(0, k - n + 1)
            j_end = min(n, k + 1)
            jj = np.arange(j_start, j_end)
            partner[jj] = a[k - jj]
            idx_ord = np.argsort(partner, kind='stable')
            j = int(idx_ord[0])
            if j == q:
                if idx_ord.size > 1:
                    j = int(idx_ord[1])
                else:
                    continue
            linW = 2.0*(float(s[j]) - float(s[q]))
            idxj=2*j; idxq=2*q; idxs=j+q
            quad = (float(p[idxj]) if idxj<m else 0.0) + ...
            tmax=float(a[q])
            if tmax <=1e-15: continue
            ts=gen_candidate_ts(linW, quad, tmax)
            if len(ts)>0:
                wmin=min_scalar_quad(W, linW, quad, ts)
                cand.append((wmin,1,q,j,ts))
    if len(cand) == 0:
        j = int(np.argmin(s))
        idx2=2*j
        pidx = float(p[idx2]) if 0 <= idx2 < m else 0.0
        lin = 2.0*(float(s[j]) - W)
        quad = W - 2.0*float(s[j]) + pidx
        ts = [0.0, 0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.5]
        wmin = min_scalar_quad(W, lin, quad, ts)
        cand = [(wmin,0,0,j,ts)]
    cand.sort(key=candidate_key)
    eval_top = 12
    best_mval = cur_m
    best_info = None
    limit = len(cand) if len(cand) < eval_top else eval_top
    for ii in range(limit):
        entry = cand[ii]
        wmin, typ, q, j, ts = entry
        if typ == 0:
            t_best, m_best = eval_fw_move(c,a,j,ts,cur_m,1.0)
        else:
            tmax = float(a[q])
            t_best, m_best = eval_pair_move(c,a,q,j,ts,cur_m,tmax)
        if m_best < best_mval - 1e-16:
            best_mval = m_best
            best_info = (typ,q,j,t_best)
    if best_info is None:
        return None
    return (best_info[0], best_info[1], best_info[2], best_info[3], best_mval)
```
Potential issue: `chars = [int(x) for x in low_idx]` uses list comp; OK. `if 0 not in chars` O(10). Good.
`np.argsort(s, kind='stable')` stable keyword available? Yes. Could just omit kind.
`np.argpartition(c, m-top_peak_cnt)[-(top_peak_cnt):]` returns unsorted. Good.
If `m - top_peak_cnt` equals m? top=0, not. If top=m, else.

```
  arr=c+t*L
```

Let's implement `eval_pair_move` with tmax param and t clipped.

```
def eval_pair_move(c,a,q,j,ts,cur_m,tmax):
    n=a.size; m=c.size
    if q==j or tmax<=1e-15: return 0.0,cur_m
    best_t=0.0; best_val=cur_m
    L=np.zeros(m)
    L[j:j+n] += 2.0*a
    L[q:q+n] -= 2.0*a
    mods=((2*j,1.0),(2*q,1.0),(j+q,-2.0))
    for t in ts:
       tt=float(t)
       if tt < 0: tt=0.0
       if tt > tmax: tt=tmax
       if tt <= 1e-15: val=cur_m
       else:
          arr = c + tt*L
          t2=tt*tt
          for idx,coef in mods:
             if 0 <= idx < m:
                arr[idx] += coef*t2
          val=float(np.max(arr))
       if val < best_val - 1e-17:
          best_val=val; best_t=tt
    if 0.0 < best_t < tmax:
       step=max(1e-8, 0.05*best_t); step=min(step, (tmax)*0.1)
       for _ in range(3):
          imp=False
          for f in (-1,-0.5,0.5,1):
             t=best_t + f*step
             if t<0: t=0
             if t>tmax: t=tmax
             if t<=1e-15: val=cur_m
             else:
                arr=c + t*L
                t2=t*t
                for idx,coef in mods: ...
                val=float(np.max(arr))
             if val < best_val - 1e-17: ...
          if not imp: break
          step*=0.4
    return best_t,best_val
```
If tmax small, step maybe 0; if step<=0 skip.

```
for idx,...:
   elapsed=time.monotonic()-t_global
```
